Cisse discusses humble beginnings, choosing Milan, Italy call-up, Amorim and amazing form

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Alphadjo Cisse has shed some light on his journey to becoming an AC Milan player, as well as what his first few months at the club have been like.

It has been a simply remarkable rise for Cisse, who at the start of 2026 was playing in the second division with Catanzaro, on loan from Hellas Verona. Milan bought him from there but left him with his loan club, before a serious injury needed surgery and ruled him out for the rest of the season.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Fast-forwarding to early November, and the 20-year-old is currently the top scorer for the Rossoneri with eight goals, the most recent of which came in Sunday’s 2-1 win over Genoa. There is talk of an imminent renewal already, while Roberto Mancini gave him a senior Italy call-up.

Cisse gave an exclusive interview to Cronache di Spogliatoio in which he touched upon various topics from his upbringing to the brilliant moment he is currently experiencing. MilanNews relayed his words.

“My life changed in my father’s factory. A Verona manager went to meet him at work to beg him to take me to their place. He’d been a worker forever: every day he’d leave work at 5:00pm, and by 5:15 he was already outside our house, calling me and taking me to play soccer.

“I still remember the first time. It was afternoon. He came up to the house and said, ‘Come on, I’ll take you somewhere’. And we went to the pitch. He was the one who instilled in me the passion for soccer, and from that moment on, I’ve never stopped.

“My parents made so many sacrifices to support me on this journey: my mom, when she had days off from work, would come up with me to take me on the bus to practice.”

“My mom is a very demanding mom. But she helps me stay focused and prevent fame from overwhelming me. So everything becomes easier with her. They tell me every day that they’re proud of me, but they also do everything they can to keep me grounded.

“Carlos, Milan’s assistant coach, often tells me this too: ‘ Surround yourself with people who truly love you and who help you stay focused’. I’m sure of this: I carry with me the carefree attitude of the child I was. I play with a smile. It’s hard not to have that in these first few months.”

You have just been called up by the Italian national team for the first time…

“I was on my way to do the pre-match interview against Ferencváros in the Europa League and they stopped me to say, ‘Alpha, they’ve called you up to the national team… the senior team!’ I had the match, I only realised afterwards.

“I want to prove I can compete. The call was in the air, they’d been talking to me about it for days… but this is my big chance. I haven’t had the chance to stop and think about my career. Football moves fast.”

“A few weeks ago, they asked me how much of a footballer I’d shown. I said 70%. Can I say that? Maybe I need to lower it a bit… let’s say 40%, because I have so many aspects to improve on, but at the same time, I know who I am and what I’m worth.

“Last season, Aquilani – who was my coach at Catanzaro – told me that ‘if you don’t play at least 10 years in Serie A, I think you’ll have failed’. I took it as a positive motivation, a phrase that pushed me to always do better and believe in what I can do.

“One of my friends in football is Favasuli, I met him just last year in Serie B. We often found ourselves talking… daydreaming. I still remember the day he said to me: ‘Think about when one day we’ll face two great teams’.

“I smiled. ‘It would be great to meet again in Serie A, but maybe it’s a little too late for me, I’m already 23…’ Instead, he made it too: Milan-Napoli will be in December, we’ll see. He was my driver, he drove me to camp. He’s proof that there’s no age to stop feeling young or believing in it, no matter the path.”

Your path has already taken you from Verona to Catanzaro and to Milan…

“In those very months, my life changed again. No longer in my father’s factory. In a matter of days, I had gone from wondering how far I was from Serie A to finding myself facing the most important decision of my career.

“I’ll be honest: I didn’t take that moment very well. When I arrived at Catanzaro, I had only played two five-minute games in Serie A, including against AC Milan. Aquilani and manager Polito really wanted me, they pushed me. It was my first experience away from home.

“In just a few months, I scored a lot, everyone wanted me. And in January, I was practically on a flight to Holland, bound for Rotterdam: to sign with PSV. But then AC Milan came along.”

“My friends’ WhatsApp group exploded: ‘Alpha, is it true?!’ And I was like, ‘Yes guys, yes!’ I didn’t want to make the wrong choice: PSV wanted me right away, while Milan would have let me finish the season in Serie B.

“Two great teams, but Milan… how do you explain that? I was experiencing a mix of emotions; my parents told me to stay calm and that the best was yet to come. We decided this was the right choice. And judging by how things are going, I’d say we were right.”

“The first few times I came to Milanello, I did so to recover from my injury. Milan left me at Catanzaro, yes, but after a few days I got injured. In fact, I had serious doubts: ‘I’m coming to AC Milan after an injury, let’s see how it goes’.

“This is exactly what Amorim spoke to me about as soon as we met. We focused on the injury, and he explained what he expected of me on the pitch. He’s a great guy, and he knows how to deal with us young players. And he knows how to keep me grounded. Even though I’m not someone who likes to let my mind wander.”

You kept a promise after your Europa League hat-trick…

“Even after my hat-trick in the Europa League, I have to say that everything was already clear to me: it was simply confirmation to myself that I can compete at this level. I didn’t see it as a point of arrival, but as a promise kept to the child I was.

“And above all, as a promise to keep. Even though I was coming back from a long injury, even though I’m young and have so many areas to improve, I never had any doubts about my abilities or where I could go. I’m very demanding of myself, just like my mother.”

“Modrić gave me some advice on what I need to improve: I need to read the game better and understand the moments of the match. He’s a great player, a spectacular person, willing to help you learn, and interesting to listen to.

“Even though my idol was very different as a kid: Paul Pogba, when he was at Juventus. We do different things on the pitch, and his haircut certainly didn’t go unnoticed by a kid… maybe I’ll get one in the future! What I appreciated about him was that he made people laugh: that’s what I want to do too.

“I was inspired by the player whose jersey I had as a child: Clarence Seedorf. My mother gave it to me; it was the one I wore on the pitch. So maybe it was just my destiny to come here. Seedorf became an idol at San Siro.

“The place where you feel the grandeur, the pressure, the feeling of playing in a stadium that represents a childhood dream. Being there now is like saying to the child I was: ‘See? You did it’. It’s a huge motivation for me. But I’m carefree enough not to be affected by it.

“When you start playing, you notice it less and you don’t pay attention: it’s easier. The fans are incredibly attached to the team; you notice it not just in Milan, but all over the world. And that’s how you understand where you are. I was really impressed by the organization here.”

Do you like to play with a certain freedom?

“I’m very carefree when I go on the pitch. Whether it’s playing in Serie B or going to San Siro, I think it’s normal for me. I’m the same person I always am. As a kid, I was obsessed with bicycle kicks; I scored five or six incredible goals… now I avoid them!

“In the Verona youth team, I had a coach, Paolo Sammarco, who shaped the way I play today: he gave me a lot of freedom, helping me understand how I should express myself.”

Is it important for young Italian players to have confidence?

“Italy is full of strong young players, I assure you. It all depends on the opportunities you’re given. Many have emerged in recent months, including me, and I’m sure many more will emerge in the near future.

“When you reach a certain level, you’re amazed: one above all, I was struck by Pisilli, he’s simply amazing. I knew him, but when I played with him, I understood his strength. And I understood my own strength when, in my early years in Verona, I approached the big boys.

“The level was rising, but I continued to have faith, to understand that I could achieve great things if I followed the path I had. That I had something special.”

Your life has changed a lot, are you still the same person?

“My life has changed so much in the past few months, perhaps more than I can even realize. There are moments when I stop and think about how different everything is from just a short while ago. Football is taking me to places I dreamed of, but inside I’m still the same boy.

“But I know what my ambitions are. And perhaps that’s exactly what helps me not lose myself, even when everything changes. I try to enjoy every moment, without forgetting where I started from and all the people who have accompanied me to this point.”

Finally, clear something up: how do you say your name?

“My name is Cisse, without an accent. My name can be read any way, but it’s pronounced ‘Sisse’. I even had to send a voice message to the commentators!”

Strategy buys 1,036 Bitcoin as BTC holdings climb to 850,364

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Strategy bought $81.9 million in Bitcoin last week while spending $188.3 million repurchasing STRC preferred shares, according to its latest SEC filing.

Strategy bought 1,036 Bitcoin for $81.9 million last week, bringing its holdings to 850,364 BTC.

The company paid an average of $79,012 per BTC last week, according to its Monday filing with the US Securities and Exchange Commission.

Strategy said it paid a total of $64.16 billion for its Bitcoin holdings, at an average price of $75,456 per coin. Its holdings now account for roughly 4.05% of Bitcoin’s maximum 21 million supply.

Since the start of October, Strategy has spent about $1.06 billion buying back its STRC preferred shares, nearly five times the $219.7 million it spent on Bitcoin over the same period.

Strategy raised $283.8 million in net proceeds last week by selling about $2.05 million shares of tis common stock (MSTR) through its at-the-market offering program.

Strategy used $188.3 million of those proceeds to repurchase about 1.89 million STRC shares, following $208.2 million in buybacks the previous week. It spent $81.9 million on Bitcoin and allocated another $13.6 million to its USD Cash balance.

Related: Strategy shareholders approve daily dividends for four preferred stocks

Strategy’s separate US dollar reserve fell to $4.77 billion from $4.79 billion after the company drew $21.1 million to pay dividends on its preferred stock.

Its USD Cash balance rose about 10% to $146.2 million from $132.7 million.

The latest Bitcoin purchase follows Strategy’s acquisition of 811 BTC for $65.5 million the previous week.

Strategy’s common stock fell 3% to $125 in Monday’s premarket trading, while its STRC preferred shares edged down 0.06% to $99.7, according to Yahoo Finance.

Apple's not the only player in the foldable business

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Foldable phones are, among other things, very expensive. Prices for the book-style foldables are so high that the cheapest models start at $1,800 — with different storage options exceeding over $3,000. Thankfully, we're now at the time of year when phone prices drop, including some of the most recent releases.

We won't be seeing any discounts on the iPhone Duo; Apple doesn't really do iPhone discounts, and the Duo is already sold out until next year. But Apple isn't the only company in the foldable market, and its rivals have already been slashing prices in the aftermath of the Duo's launch. Not all of them are recent releases, but with the slow pace of phone upgrades these days, that's not necessarily a bad thing. Especially if it means saving a bunch of cash.

So if you're in the market for a foldable phone, and aren't choosy about which brand you buy, here are the best early Black Friday foldable phone deals available right now.

If you're looking for a passport-shaped foldable, with a similar look to the iPhone Duo, look no further than the Galaxy Z Fold 8. Samsung's offering has a similar look and feel, with a pair of 50MP cameras on the back. It's also thinner and lighter than the iPhone, with a more colorful anti-glare display. However, it lacks some features like stylus support and proper dust resistance.This isn't the cheapest we've seen the Z Fold 8 go for, but it's still a generous discount from the normal price.

If you want a larger foldable, then the Galaxy Z Fold 8 Ultra is easily one of the best foldable phones you can buy. It comes with all the same benefits as the Z Fold 8, but in a larger book-style design. It also packs a more powerful 200MP camera, a telephoto lens and a larger 5,000 mAh battery.

The Pixel 11 Pro Fold isn't the most advanced foldable, especially in terms of the display crease and battery life, but it does combine all the benefits of a folding device with the things that make a Pixel great. This means you get a large, bright display, some of the best AI features of any phone, and good cameras. Plus early access to all the latest Android updates as they happen.

If you want a cheap foldable in a more compact form, then the Motorola Razr (2025) is one of your best options. The newer Razr 2026 model is not on sale right now, but the 2025 model does offer a lot. You get a bright 6.9-inch display, a suite of AI features, a 120Hz refresh rate on both screens and a large cover display that can run just about any Android app you like.

If you want something a lot more capable, the 2025 Razr Ultra is almost half price ahead of Black Friday. This comes with the same 6.9-inch display as the standard Razr, but ups the refresh rate all the way to a crazy 165Hz. It also comes with 512GB storage as standard, 16GB of RAM, a pair of 50MP main cameras, a 50MP selfie camera and a whopping 4,500 nits of display brightness.

TRON Network Integrates with Backpack Wallet, Expanding Access to Its Multichain Ecosystem

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Geneva, Switzerland, November 9, 2026 — TRON DAO, the community-governed DAO dedicated to accelerating the decentralization of the internet through blockchain technology and decentralized applications (dApps), today announced the integration of the TRON network with Backpack Wallet. The integration brings TRON’s blockchain ecosystem directly to Backpack users, enabling them to send, receive, bridge, and manage TRON-based digital assets through Backpack’s mobile app and browser extension, all while maintaining full self-custody.

Through the integration, Backpack users will be able to send, receive, and hold TRON-based digital assets, including USDT (TRC-20), directly within their wallet while maintaining full self-custody. Users will also be able to bridge assets between TRON and other supported blockchains, explore decentralized finance applications and dApps across the TRON ecosystem, including Sun.io, and manage their TRON assets alongside holdings from other supported networks within a single multichain wallet experience.

This integration follows the recent listing of TRX for spot trading and perpetual contracts on Backpack Exchange. The listings expanded market access to TRON’s native utility token, allowing users in eligible jurisdictions to trade TRX through both spot and perpetual markets on Backpack’s trading platform. Together, these developments establish a foundation for deeper ecosystem connectivity, bringing TRON’s blockchain infrastructure and digital assets to Backpack users across both its wallet and exchange products.

The collaboration reinforces TRON’s commitment to expanding global access to blockchain infrastructure, stablecoin payments, and decentralized finance, while advancing its mission to make digital assets more accessible worldwide.

TRON DAO is a community-governed DAO dedicated to accelerating the decentralization of the internet via blockchain technology and dApps.

Founded in September 2017, the TRON blockchain has experienced significant growth since its MainNet launch in May 2018. Today, TRON hosts the largest circulating supply of USD Tether (USDT) stablecoin, which currently exceeds $94 billion. As of November 2026, the TRON blockchain has recorded over 412 million in total user accounts, more than 16 billion in total transactions, and over $27 billion in total value locked (TVL), based on TRONSCAN. Recognized as the global settlement layer for stablecoin transactions and everyday purchases with proven success, TRON is “Moving Trillions, Empowering Billions.”

Subcompact SUVs May Seem Appealing, but Compact Cars Are Often a Better Buy

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Unless you’re dead-set on the tall seating position that an SUV provides, we think these small hatchbacks are far more satisfying than their crossover siblings.

Sure, SUVs are popular, but that doesn’t mean they’re always the best choice for your needs. The smaller an SUV gets, the fewer advantages it offers when compared with an equivalent sedan or hatchback. Here, we’re looking at the subcompact SUV segment and stacking up these small crossovers against compact cars from the same brand.

Subcompact SUVs are the smallest of their species. As the name suggests, they’re smaller than compact SUVs, and their tidier footprints often mean compromises in space, fuel economy, and driving satisfaction.

Automakers feel the need to offer additional ground clearance and all-wheel drive in these SUV models to justify their categorization. But these models typically share the same underpinnings and engines as their compact-car kin.

This means these SUV models are often heavier, less enjoyable to drive, and less fuel-efficient than their non-SUV siblings. Both types of vehicles also typically offer similar amounts of cargo and passenger space. Pricing, meanwhile, tends to favor the compact hatchbacks.

If these two types of vehicles are so similar, then why the different classifications? Blame size creep.

Cars and SUVs typically get larger with each passing generation. Let’s roll the clock back 25 years. While most modern compact cars are bigger than their counterparts from a quarter century ago, they still tend to cast smaller shadows than mid-size cars from the turn of the millennium.

On the other hand, the subcompact SUVs of today are typically larger than the compact SUVs of 25 years ago. Due to this, calling a modern SUV “subcompact” better describes its size relative to the overall SUV landscape as opposed to the larger passenger-vehicle space.

Odds are that if you’re shopping for one of these small-sized SUVs, you’re going to be just as well—if not better—served by these vehicles’ often less expensive and more fun-to-drive compact hatchback stablemates.

The HR-V is Honda's smallest SUV, but it’s still plenty spacious inside. The main downside of this model is that it’s available with only one powertrain: a naturally aspirated 2.0-liter inline-four that produces just 158 horsepower.

This means the HR-V accelerates at a leisurely pace. In our testing, this little Honda SUV needed 9.4 seconds to hit 60 mph.

The HR-V earns an EPA-estimated combined rating of 28 mpg in front-drive form. Adding all-wheel drive drops that figure to 27 mpg.

The Honda Civic hatchback has the same base engine as the CR-V, but it also offers a more powerful and more fuel-efficient hybrid variant that has quicker acceleration and much better fuel economy—up to 49 mpg combined, according to the EPA. The Civic doesn’t offer all-wheel drive as the HR-V does, but we’d advocate for a good set of winter tires instead.

In our testing, the Civic hatchback’s cargo area was only marginally smaller than the HR-V’s. It held six carry-on suitcases behind the rear seat, which is only one fewer case than the HR-V can accommodate.

More importantly, the Civic is far more fun to drive than the HR-V.

A redesign for 2027 brings eye-catching new looks to the Kia Seltos subcompact SUV. A 147-hp four-cylinder comes standard, while a 190-hp turbo four is optional. For its second generation, the Seltos also offers an available gas-electric hybrid powertrain—an option the Kia K4 compact hatch lacks.

Sure, the Kia K4 hatchback doesn’t offer hybrid power. However, what this handsome hatch lacks in powertrain options, it makes up for in other ways.

Simply put, we prefer the way the K4 drives to the Seltos. Credit the compact hatchback’s lower center of gravity, which improves handling and behind-the-wheel engagement.

The K4 is also more miserly than the equivalent gas-only Seltos. When fitted with the available 190-hp turbocharged 1.6-liter four-cylinder, the K4 hatch is also notably quicker than its equally powerful Seltos kin. In our testing, a so-equipped K4 hatch accelerated to 60 mph in 7.0 seconds, while a turbocharged Seltos needed 8.2 seconds to hit the same mark.

We haven’t yet measured how many carry-on suitcases fit in the cargo bay of the new Seltos. Compared to the previous-generation Seltos, though, the K4 hatch fell one carry-on suitcase short of its subcompact SUV sibling’s sum.

The Subaru Crosstrek is the spiritual successor to the old Impreza Outback Sport, which was essentially an Impreza hatchback with added body cladding. Compared to the old Outback Sport, the Crosstrek is a bit more distinct than the Impreza it’s based on. Even so, the Crosstrek is still effectively a lifted Impreza hatch with additional ground clearance and more rugged looks. As such, we don’t think the Crosstrek is worth the extra cost over the run-of-the-mill Impreza hatchback that Subaru still offers.

Subaru makes an even better case for the Impreza hatch for 2027 courtesy of a new base model that starts at less than $26,000. As we previously detailed, the Impreza beats the Crosstrek in nearly every metric in our testing: It’s quicker, more fuel-efficient, and offers as much space inside. The only reason we can think of to choose the Crosstrek is for its available gas-electric hybrid powertrain or its extra ground clearance.

The Mazda CX-30 is among our favorite subcompact crossover SUVs. It drives great, features a high-quality interior, and provides sufficient oomph with the standard 186-hp 2.5-liter four-cylinder engine. Opt for the 250-hp turbocharged 2.5-liter, and the all-wheel-drive CX-30 accelerates with even more vigor. But what if Mazda also sold a vehicle that paired all the positive traits of the CX-30 with a lower starting price, even better driving dynamics, and an available manual gearbox? Well, good news: It does.

The Mazda 3 hatchback may do without the higher seating position of the CX-30, but it makes up for it with livelier dynamics courtesy of its lower center of gravity and lighter curb weight. At a manufacturer-estimated 3111 pounds, the front-drive 3 hatch’s mass undercuts that of the all-wheel-drive CX-30 by 284 pounds.

While we think the available six-speed manual gearbox of the front-drive 3 makes it superior to its all-wheel-drive kin, we know not everyone will agree with us. No worries, though, because Mazda also offers the 3 with all-wheel drive. Opting for it adds 172 pounds to the curb weight of 3 hatchbacks with the standard 186-hp four. However, AWD is standard on turbocharged models.

In our testing, both the 3 hatchback and CX-30 were able to hold up to 16 carry-on suitcases with the rear seats folded. With the rear seats in place, the 3 hatch’s cargo area held five carry-on suitcases—just one fewer than the CX-30.

Acura’s subcompact SUV is about as interesting and exciting as watching C-SPAN. Short of its available all-wheel-drive system and slightly bigger interior compartment, there are few other reasons to pick the ADX over its compact hatchback sibling, the Integra.

With a starting price of $34,795, the Acura Integra undercuts the ADX by a significant $1755. It’s also significantly more enjoyable to drive and a good deal quicker.

Though both the Integra and ADX come standard with a turbocharged 1.5-liter four-cylinder engine that mates to a continuously variable automatic transmission (CVT), the Integra benefits from an extra 10 horsepower and 13 pound-feet of torque relative to the unit in the ADX. With 200 horsepower and 192 pound-feet of torque, a CVT-equipped Integra accelerated to 60 mph in a reasonable 7.1 seconds. An all-wheel-drive ADX, meanwhile, required a languid 8.7 ticks to hit 60 mph.

Admittedly, the fastback shape of the Integra cuts into its cargo-carrying capabilities. We fit 18 carry-on suitcases in its cargo bay with the rear seats folded and five with the seatbacks up. The squarer shape of the ADX afforded totals of 22 and seven, respectively.

Despite being raised on a steady diet of base-model Hondas and Toyotas—or perhaps because of it—Joey Capparella cultivated an obsession for the automotive industry throughout his childhood in Nashville, Tennessee. He found a way to write about cars for the school newspaper during his college years at Rice University, which eventually led him to move to Ann Arbor, Michigan, for his first professional auto-writing gig at Automobile Magazine. He has been part of the Hearst Autos team since 2016 and now lives in New York City.

Despite their shared last name, Greg Fink is not related to Ed "Big Daddy" Roth's infamous Rat Fink. Both Finks, however, are known for their love of cars, car culture, and—strangely—monogrammed one-piece bathing suits. Greg's career in the media industry goes back more than a decade. His previous experience includes stints as an editor at publications such as U.S. News & World Report, The Huffington Post, Motor1.com, and MotorTrend.

At $8,000, Could This 1977 Cadillac Seville Milan Get You To Go Short?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Custom-built by Millan Coach Builders of Simi Valley, California, today's Nice Price or No Dice Cadillac Milan is one of around 500 converted and truncated two-seaters. Let's see if its price will make short business of securing a sale.

For nearly a decade and a half in the 1980s and '90s, Chrysler's K-Car served as the basis for over three-quarters of the company's automotive output. During that span, the humble FWD platform spawned nearly fifty different models, encompassing family cars, wagons, sports cars, limos, and Chrysler's savior minivans.

We looked at an amazingly clean example of one of those models last Friday: a 1986 Chrysler LeBaron Town & Country with fewer than 4,000 miles. It was a total throwback, and in beautiful shape. Still, nice as it was, few of you wanted to step into that time machine, especially at its $18,900 asking price. Ultimately, the comments dunned the LeBaron as just a crappy K Car, with the voting going likewise in an 82% 'No Dice' loss.

In antiquity, Milan, Italy, then known as Mediolanum, served as the capital of the Western Roman Empire. Today it serves as the locus of both Italy's automotive and fashion industries. With all that history, it's surprising that today's 1977 Cadillac Seville Milan isn't named for the Lombardy capital.

Instead, both the car and the company, Milan Coach Builders, were named for the car's designer and company founder, Milan Daniels. Built in small numbers (reportedly fewer than 500), the Milan was not sanctioned by Cadillac or GM.

Private parties would bring their already-purchased Sevilles to Milan's shop, where the cars were cut in half, shrunk from a 114-inch wheelbase to 96 inches, and fitted with a convertible top. Lost in the process were the roof, rear doors, and the back seat. At the time, the idea was to create an American analog to the Mercedes SL, a model that the Milan's dimensions purposely aped. Optionally, Milan Coach Builders also offered a fiberglass hood with a central bump, and an accompanying Rolls-style Parthenon grille.

The result looks less like a Mercedes SL and more like Internet sensation and beloved role model for overcoming adversity, Seattle's Jimothy the Raccoon. That's not a bad association, and considering that upwards of 500 people sent their then-very-expensive Cadillacs to go under the knife, it's obvious these cars have some appeal.

The conversion was only offered on the first-generation Sevilles, and Milan Coach Builders shuttered in the early 1980s after that model ceased production. This one, in white over a white leather interior and charcoal canvas top, is claimed to be in decent condition.

According to the ad's description, it also has had a 6.2-liter Chevy LS3 V8 professionally installed, just 108 miles ago. Overall, the car reportedly has 30,906 miles on the clock. That engine, the seller assures us, provides the car with both a "massive power upgrade and turn key reliability." The seller also boasts that "The shortened wheelbase makes this incredibly nimble compared to a standard Seville," and says that "it is a blast to drive." Aside from the modern Chevy mill, this Milan seems to otherwise maintain its '70s groove.

Aesthetically, the seller comments that the car is in great condition, has been "meticulously cared for and shows beautifully." That's interesting, since the photos in the ad, which appear to have been taken with a pinhole camera by a distracted three-year-old, show several discrepancies. In one shot of the car with the top down—arguably its most flattering pose—a rub strip spans the passenger door, and a bumper gap filler sits at the rear. In another photo showing the car with the top up, both the strip and the filler are absent. That bumper gap filler (made of urethane) is also absent in the trunk shot, as are the trunk lock and Cadillac wreath.

It's hard to say which of those photos represents the car in its current state, although the seller claims the Caddy "shows beautifully," so perhaps the photos showing missing parts are from a previous state, and the car is now in its final, intact form. One thing we do know, from the ad, is that this Milan comes with a clean title.

It's always smart to hedge your bets, and the seller of this Milan has done just that by listing the car on both Craigslist and Facebook Marketplace. The Craigslist ad has been up for about three weeks, which isn't unreasonable for such a unique vehicle seeking an appreciative buyer. The Facebook Marketplace ad, however, has been up for 32 weeks, which shows what a backwater the Meta classifieds can sometimes be. The main discrepancy between the ads, and the likely reason that the seller managed no bites on Facebook, is that there the car is priced at $16,000, while on Craigslist it's asking half that: $8,000. We're going with the Craigslist price, so it's time to weigh in and vote on whether that's a good deal.

What's your take on this Milan and that $8,000 asking price? Does that feel fair given the car's unique history and presentation? Or, even at that lower-than-Facebook price, does this custom Caddy come up short?

San Francisco Bay Area, California, Craigslist, or go here if the ad disappears.

Help me out with Nice Price or No Dice. Hit me up at robemslie@gmail.com and send a fixed-price tip. Remember to include your commenter handle.

Strategy Buys $82M in Bitcoin, Buys Back $188M of STRC

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Strategy Buys $82M in Bitcoin, Buys Back $188M of STRC

Strategy bought 1,036 BTC for $81.9 million last week, its second straight weekly purchase, the company said on Monday.

The Bitcoin treasury firm paid an average of $79,012 per coin between November 2 and November 8, according to an SEC filing. It now holds 850,364 BTC, acquired for a total of $64.16 billion at an average of about $75,456 per coin.

Strategy has acquired 1,036 $BTC and repurchased $188M of $STRC. As of 11/8/26, we hold 850,364 BTC and $4.9B of USD Assets. $MSTRhttps://t.co/dNzTPPjday

The purchase is larger than the week before, when Strategy resumed buying with 811 BTC for $65.5 million after a one-week pause.

Strategy also bought back 1,887,464 shares of STRC, its variable-rate preferred stock, for $188.3 million, down from $208.2 million the previous week. It funded both the Bitcoin and the buyback by selling 2,051,348 shares of MSTR stock for $283.8 million in net proceeds, with the remaining $13.6 million going to its USD Cash account.

That marks a change from last week, when Strategy paid for its STRC buyback from USD Cash, drawing the balance down to $132.7 million. With the new proceeds, USD Cash rose to $146.2 million as of November 8.

Strategy's separate USD Reserve, which covers dividends on its preferred stock and interest on its debt, stood at $4.77 billion after it paid out $21.1 million in preferred dividends during the week. About $560.3 million remains available under its preferred stock repurchase program, and $1 billion under its program for buying back MSTR stock.

The update comes two days before index provider MSCI is due to publish the list of companies affected by its new rules on firms dominated by a single asset, which could remove Strategy from its equity indexes.

Strategy's preferred stock is also adjusting to a new payment schedule. Shareholders approved daily dividends on its four preferred stocks last month, starting with STRC on November 1.

17 of the best quotes on Alessandro Del Piero: ‘He feels it in his soul’

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

As an icon of Juventus and Italian football in general throughout the 1990s and 2000s, footballers don’t come much cooler than Alessandro Del Piero.

Over 19 years at Juventus, Del Piero made 704 appearances, scored 289 goals and won Serie A six times and the Champions League once.

With Italy he scored 27 goals in 91 caps and was a key member of the squad which won the 2006 World Cup.

But Del Piero truly earned his status as a footballing legend in Turin, specifically in his devotion to the Bianconeri following their demotion to Serie B in 2006.

While many players left, Del Piero remained at Juve to captain the side back to the top flight and eventually back to the title.

Fittingly nicknamed Il Pinturicchio in reference to the great renaissance artist, we’ll always love Del Piero, but we’ve left it to those who know him best to explain why he is such a legend.

“Del Piero is the best player in the history of Italian football. He is a person for who I do not only have a lot of respect but a lot of affection as well.”

“He’s the greatest player I’ve ever played against. He dribbled in an incredible way.”

“In my view he’s the best person I’ve met in this sport.”

“Alex … what an opponent! What a player! What a person! A real symbol, a champion who gave trophies to the Juventus fans, he loves that shirt and gives lots of back pain to opposing goalkeepers.

“Alessa, I think the older I become the more scared I am of facing you on the field. You are one of the best in the game, I admire you and respect you.”

READ: Alessandro Del Piero, a Juventus icon who delivered everything he promised

“He is different to Zinedine Zidane. He likes to play, he feels it in his soul. Between him and the Frenchman, I choose him.”

“Del Piero has been an inspiration for me throughout my career.”

“Maldini, Del Piero and Raul are all the symbols of world football.”

“It’s difficult to find words to describe Del Piero. He has been playing for Juve for about 20 years ‒ he is the history of this club. When anybody in the world says the word ‘Juve’, they have a picture of Del Piero in mind. I think that says it all.”

“Del Piero is the best Italian footballer.”

“Regarding Del Piero’s situation, my thoughts are very clear. I would sum it up in just four words: Del Piero is Juventus.”

“Del Piero is my idol. When I arrived at Juventus and I was introduced to Alex, it was like touching the heavens with my finger.”

“Alessandro Del Piero was the symbol of the club for many years.”

“He always comes to the training field with a smile for everyone, a comforting word for everyone. This is his greatness: humbleness… he’s a golden person.”

“Vieri is a player I would like at United. But maybe Del Piero would be even better. He would be perfect for us.”

“After his penultimate appearance in a Juventus jersey, he was finally overwhelmed by all the emotions he’d managed to keep a lid on up till then. His ego, his desire to be involved, that need to feel like a true Bianconero.

“He flooded the dressing room with tears, and we did the same, both with him and for him.”

“From tomorrow I will no longer be a Juventus player, but I will always be one of you.”

“A true gentleman never leaves his lady.”

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/digital-health/ai-enabled-virtual-mental-health-startup-siggy-launches-stealth" hreflang="en">AI-enabled virtual mental health startup Siggy launches from stealth</a>

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →
AI-enabled virtual mental health startup Siggy launches from stealth" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Siggy, an AI-enabled virtual mental health provider, has launched out of stealth with $6 million in pre-seed funding.

Siggy aims to build a complete mental health system that delivers continuous, personalized care. It combines therapy, prescription management and AI for intake and between-visits support. Intake is free; patients only pay if they accept treatment. Siggy is backed by Village Global, alongside several angel investors.

Patients begin with a free, anonymous conversation with Siggy’s AI that doesn’t require an account or login. It remembers everything, so patients don’t have to repeat their history when they eventually see a provider. An AI-powered preliminary diagnosis is reviewed by clinicians, and a patient is presented with treatment options. After a call with a human provider, patients log into Siggy to add existing providers or relatives to the system and confirm their treatment goals.

If a patient accepts treatment, they see a human clinician monthly with the option of engaging with Siggy’s AI between visits, which draws on their full history. Siggy’s therapy AI agent does not answer medical questions, instead directing them to human providers. It can help patients work through their goals, remind them of specific exercises and be a general sounding board. There are also safety protocols in place to flag higher-acuity needs to human providers.

Siggy can connect to wearables and offers a one-minute daily check-in on sleep, mood, how the body feels and medication side effects. These data combined form the Siggy Score, a single number out of 100 that patients and their clinicians see every day. When Siggy sees a pattern building, it flags the change to the patient’s clinician with the context behind it. Weekly check-ins in the first two months of treatment let clinicians decide whether to hold a dose, increase it or change a medication.

“It can build a really detailed picture of you rather fast, and the idea is if there are behavior patterns that are recurrent…[Siggy can] begin to help people gain agency and recognize them before they happen,” Siggy co-founder and CEO Jonathan Sabbagh told Fierce Healthcare.

Sabbagh co-founded Siggy with Edvard Engesaeth, M.D., now COO, an early Journey Clinical investor who co-founded Nurx. Siggy’s chief medical officer is Daniel Montville, M.D., a Mayo Clinic-trained psychiatrist. Sabbagh previously worked for two decades in finance before finding out he was living with chronic burnout and undiagnosed PTSD from childhood trauma.

Sabbagh sought psychedelic-assisted therapy and went back to school to study psychology before dropping out to co-found Journey Clinical, a large psychedelic-assisted therapy network in the U.S. with over 3,000 practices. “I saw a real gap where people drop off treatments, they don’t have continuous care,” Sabbagh told Fierce Healthcare.

About 16% of American adults take antidepressants, and a fifth annually drop out before completing the recommended course of treatment. “Prescriptions aren’t the problem … it really is a fragmented system where you don’t have therapists ever speaking with your prescriber,” Sabbagh said.

Siggy can prescribe medications, except controlled substances. Clinicians will taper patients off medications when they are ready. Longer-term, Siggy hopes to be able to identify other conditions through analytics on speech, labs, genetic testing and biomarkers, such as diabetes or ALS. It also hopes to build out neural datasets to support clinical trials for technologies like brain implants.

Siggy is covered by major commercial payers in all 50 states, including Blue Cross Blue Shield, Aetna, UnitedHealthcare and Humana. It also takes Medicaid, Medicare, TRICARE and is free for veterans.

“Our goal is to provide continuous care for life. We don’t really see meds as a feature. It is a combination of building persistent AI identity, being able to track progress and to make essentially personalized decisions that very few could afford,” Sabbagh said.

Alessandro Del Piero, a Juventus icon who delivered on his promise

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

As counter-intuitive as it might feel, Alessandro Del Piero’s clinching goal in the 2006 World Cup semi-final is both representative of his quality and unrepresentative of his career.

The Italian forward didn’t need to lift the ball into the top corner of Jens Lehmann’s net in the final minute of extra-time.

Time was up, and Fabio Grosso had won the game for Italy – the final whistle would surely blow before Germany had any chance to get the ball down the other end – but that didn’t really matter, did it?

Del Piero achieved a great deal during his time with Juventus, taking over as the club’s top scorer in that same year, and left the Italian giants holding the record by more than 100 goals – a margin which still exists to this day.

However, his presence as an effective one-club man – there were a couple of seasons elsewhere in his teens and late 30s – and some relatively late achievements in an Italy shirt means he perhaps only now gets the wider plaudits he ought to have earned during his playing career.

Roberto Baggio, Christian Vieri and even Francesco Totti thrived for the Azzurri at major tournaments, but Del Piero’s role was less prominent. As such, there may have been a feeling among some fans that he belonged much more to Juve than to his country.

That said, Del Piero’s achievements for the Bianconeri were very special indeed, and when you return to one of his best goals for the club you realise how much sense the strike against Germany made.

Less than five minutes into a Champions League meeting with Real Madrid, Del Piero ran onto a pass from Vincenzo Iaquinta and found himself staring down the throat of Madrid goalkeeper Iker Casillas, but his version of staring down someone’s throat equated to landing M&Ms in the mouth of a sleeping colleague on the other side of the office.

You’d argue he took the shot too early if it wasn’t for that element of surprise almost certainly being responsible for the ball hitting the net before Casillas was able to move his feet.

It’s the same approach that helped him beat Lehmann in 2006, recognising there was no need to find out whether he had the yards in his legs when he could bypass the extra distance and still achieve what he wanted.

Del Piero vs. Real Madrid: Brilliant passing move and an even better strike past Casillas in his prime. Beautiful. pic.twitter.com/RfuF6co7s9

— ⌬ Christian Candler (@candlerneuro) December 29, 2017

This was a man who couldn’t have been much more sure of his abilities if he tried, be it in Europe or in Serie A, scoring six goals during Juve’s run to Champions League glory in 1996 and following that up with some huge domestic seasons.

His 200-plus goals in a Juve shirt include 16 separate double-figure seasons and five in which he hit more than 15 in the league alone.

These weren’t all simple finishes, either – his 22 free-kick goals is a tally bettered only by Andrea Pirlo and Sinisa Mihajlovic, despite him having the likes of Zinedine Zidane and Pavel Nedved for company, yet he’s not considered a set-piece merchant to the same degree as the two men ahead of him in the standings.

One of the 22 came against Roma, just 11 days after the strike against Real Madrid, and left Doni with the same expression of defeat he imparted on the face of hundreds of goalkeepers over more than two decades.

It’s a mirage of a shot, one which looks within touching distance throughout, only for the keeper to find out in the most painful way that it never was.

Back to that goal against Germany, and – like so many of the other stand-out finishes – it’s easy to forget that it arrived in the second half of Del Piero’s career.

By the time he raced onto Luca Toni’s lay-off, he had played more than 300 times in Serie A and had more than 75 international caps to his name.

Indeed, that proved his final goal for his country, bringing up a total of 27 which feels too low and yet higher than you might have imagined.

Just three of Del Piero’s Italy goals came at major summer tournaments, and the strike against Germany was the only one to arrive in a knockout game.

This reinforces the Juve over Italy dichotomy and might offer an explanation if your own recollection of Del Piero does not leave him in the upper echelons of your memories.

Alessandro Del Piero might not have been the man at international level, and might not even have been the main man for his club at every single moment, but there’s a reason why he’s remembered so fondly in the city where he promised and delivered everything he could.

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

By Oliver Thomas | 09 Nov 2026 13:19

European giants Paris Saint-Germain, Chelsea and Manchester City are reportedly among a big list of clubs chasing Lens defender Pierre Ismaelo Ganiou.

Reigning Champions League winners PSG already have a wealth of defensive options at their disposal, but finding a long-term successor to captain Marquinhos, 32, may soon become a priority, prompting the club to keep a close eye on France's brightest emerging talents.

Chelsea and Man City, meanwhile, have both had their fair share of defensive issues this season, conceding 29 goals combined after 10 Premier League games, and may also consider delving into the market for backline reinforcements.

According to TEAMtalk, Chelsea owners BlueCo and City Football Group, the parent company of Man City, have been keeping tabs on 21-year-old Ganiou, while PSG are ‘fully aware’ of his potential.

Tottenham Hotspur, Newcastle United, Brentford and Fulham are said to be among the other English clubs to have watched the highly-rated centre-back this calendar year.

Ligue 1 duo Marseille and Monaco are also monitoring Ganiou progress, with Italian clubs Atalanta BC and Como, and Bundesliga pair Borussia Dortmund and RB Leipzig among the other European teams keeping a close eye on his development.

Ganiou has enhanced his reputation with Lens following a breakthrough period in French football, making his Ligue 1 debut in October 2025.

Widely regarded as Lens’ greatest-ever academy product, Ganiou is a physical, pacey and elegant centre-back who has been compared to former France and Real Madrid defender Raphael Varane.

The young defender's path to the first team was somewhat unconventional, involving temporary stints away from Lens at Annecy and Padova to bypass strict loan registration rules.

However, following his successful return, Ganiou made a massive impression during his debut season with Lens last season, with his rapid breakthrough convincing the club's hierarchy to reward him with a four-year contract extension over the summer.

Ganiou has made a total of 37 senior appearances for Lens in all competitions, with 29 of those coming in a memorable 2025-26 campaign that saw Les Sang et Or finish second in Ligue 1 and win the Coupe de France for the first time in the club's history.

The 6ft 1in defender worked under current Crystal Palace manager Pierre Sage last season and has since established himself as a first-team regular, despite the early departure of Dino Toppmoller and arrival of Yannick Cahuzac as the club’s new head coach in September.

Valued at €30m (£25m) by transfermarkt and capped five times by France Under-21s, Ganiou is currently sidelined with a knee injury, but he hopes to be back in the first-team fold soon and continue his impressive trajectory as a core component of the Lens defence while interested suitors continue to watch on.

Forget Lacey: It's Time for Carrick to Hand Zirkzee a Man Utd Chance

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Manchester United have entered the November international break in the Premier League relegation zone, after they went yet another game without a victory.

The Red Devils fought back from 2-0 down and 4-2 down to claim a 4-4 draw at Old Trafford against Aston Villa on Sunday, meaning they have won just one of their ten Premier League games this season and have just 8 points so far, leaving them in 18th place in the table.

But despite that awful form, manager Michael Carrick looks set to keep his job as manager, with INEOS having handed him a vote of confidence following the defeat to Bournemouth last month.

Since then, United have suffered a 3-0 defeat to Chelsea, beaten Roma 3-2, and drawn with Villa, and while the performances have been much improved in some areas, defensively the team have been awful. The 30 goals they have conceded in all competitions is the joint-most of any team in Europe's top five leagues.

But going forward, the team have struggled to score goals all season too. In all competitions so far, United have scored 24 goals in 15 games, but 16 of those goals have come in four games against Ipswich, Sabah, Roma, and Aston Villa.

In recent games, the team has been playing more free-flowing football, and that has seen more chances created and led to more goals being scored. But the players are still not performing too well, and they're banking on volume rather than clear-cut-chances.

Youngster Shea Lacey has been pushing hard for a start, with right-winger Amad underperforming and without a goal from open play in almost an entire calendar year. Carrick has so far stuck with the Ivorian, but there is now another forward pushing for a start.

Netherlands international Joshua Zirkzee looked set to be on the move away from Old Trafford in the summer, despite an impressive pre-season campaign. A move never worked out for him, and he has found himself sidelined by the manager for much of the season, appearing for just 186 minutes so far in all competitions.

But the tall centre-forward has been making the most out of recent substitute appearances. He got a consolation goal in the thrashing against Como in the Champions League, and he got the third goal late on against Villa too, while also making a great impact as a substitute in the draw against Leeds.

Zirkzee's time at Man Utd hasn't been great, and a January exit still seems to be more likely than him staying for the entirety of the season. But it's clear that he has earned himself an opportunity to get a start.

Former Premier League champion Lee Sharpe has sung his praises recently too, and it's clear that his technical attributes can help to bring the best out of those around him.

“Joshua Zirkzee is one of my favourite players to watch. His ability on the ball is phenomenal: in tight areas, he's got great feet and the ability to go past people."

With Benjamin Sesko ruled out with an injury until at least the new year, the lack of a natural striker has been an issue for the manager to contend with. But Zirkzee has proven he can step in and score goals, while also linking with the other forwards in the squad.

There is no reason for him to not be involved more often after the international break, and Carrick arguably owes him his job now too.

Manchester United head coach Michael Carrick has defended Diogo Dalot amid the latest round of fierce criticism of the Portugal international.

Dalot has often played out of position at left-back this season, and it has been a tough campaign for the 27-year-old, who has been a part of a defence that has struggled badly this term.

The full-back was substituted in the second half of Sunday's game with Aston Villa, and his withdrawal proved to be a popular decision with the Man United fans.

A number of Red Devils supporters revealed their displeasure at Dalot's performance when he was removed in the 66th minute.

Dalot gave away a penalty against Villa and was arguably at fault for the first goal that Man United conceded during what was a chaotic Premier League fixture.

There is currently a host of speculation surrounding Dalot's future, with the Portuguese potentially leaving Old Trafford during this summer's transfer window.

However, Carrick has come to the defence of the full-back.

“We've got to be careful with that, I think. I understand the frustration. I understand the disappointment of the supporters, so I get it. We're all frustrated. We're all disappointed. And the players are as much as anyone, I guarantee you that," Carrick told reporters.

"Diogo gives absolutely everything. He's played in different positions. He's played left-back. He's played really well at times. He gives a penalty away today. That's, you know… he knows that.

"Maybe he can do a little bit better. Alright, that's part of it, he's made a mistake. But he cares an awful lot, and he's given everything. So I like what he brings to the team.

"I like what he brings to the group. I like what he brings to the whole environment and the culture that we've got. Picking the team and who plays the next game, that's a whole different decision. But it's a whole different game that we've got to look forward to now.”

The Portugal international has represented Man United on 260 occasions since arriving from Porto in 2018, scoring 10 goals and registering 20 assists, and he has featured 14 times in all competitions during the 2026-27 campaign.

Dalot has a contract at Man United until June 2028, with the option of a further year, but both parties need to cut ties during next summer's transfer window.

The Red Devils could sign as many as four new defenders in 2027, and Dalot will not be short of interest, with the likes of AC Milan, Juventus and Inter Milan all thought to be keen.

Cardano Hits Epoch 666: Why Is It So Significant?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Cardano (ADA) has entered epoch 666, yet another milestone for the blockchain, which has been live since its launch in September 2017.

The number has drawn attention for its connection to the biblical 666, but the development also points to the Cardano network's basic timekeeping and staking mechanism.

Popular Cardano blockchain explorer Cexplorer displays that epoch 666 started on Nov. 5, 2026, and is expected to end on Nov. 10, with 1 day and 10 hours left to close. The milestone marks another five-day cycle finished in Cardano's long-running proof-of-stake system.

Cardano divides its operation into epochs, which are five days long and consist of 432,000 one-second slots. The importance of epoch 666 is largely symbolic and not technical, as it does not coincide with any major protocol upgrades or hard forks. But the number 666 has merely provided an interesting talking point for the Cardano community.

In a separate milestone, the Cardano network has processed over 127,000,000 transactions on mainnet. According to Cexplorer data, Cardano has recorded a total of 127,203,164 transactions.

In upgrade news, DijkstraNet, the Dijkstra hard fork testnet, has been hard forked to protocol version 12 and is now available for testing and integration against the full Dijkstra feature set, with the exception of Linear Leios.

Node 11.2 was pre-released on October 28 and successfully used to transition DijkstraNet into the Dijkstra era, and DB Sync 13.8.0.0 was released for all networks.

In separate upgrade news, Midnight has completed its upgrade from Ledger 8 to Ledger 9, bringing new capabilities for developers alongside security, compatibility, and performance improvements across the network.

Ledger 9 introduces native support for widely used cryptographic standards including ECDSA and Keccak-256, expands wallet and custody compatibility, and enables contracts to interact directly with one another.

Keccak-256 is the standard algorithm that Ethereum uses to scramble data into fixed-length cryptographic fingerprints. ECDSA is the standard digital signature method that networks like Bitcoin and Ethereum use to prove ownership of a specific wallet address.

Jito’s Solana data feed puts a price on seeing trades early

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Solana traders can pay to see transactions earlier through BAM without gaining the right to change their order within a committed batch. The product turns information timing into a saleable service, even as its execution rules protect the sequence of an already scheduled batch.

Helius's current subscription terms and BAM's validator revenue rules show how that earlier view is priced and who earns from selling it.

A BAM preconfirmation appears only after the leader has accepted a scheduled batch and committed to executing it in order. Unscheduled pending transactions are excluded. The stream therefore reveals committed activity earlier than a shred-based feed, rather than opening a window to rearrange that same batch.

BAM announced the service on Sept. 9, 2026 through Helius and Triton. CryptoSlate's July 2025 BAM coverage concerned the proposed sequencing architecture; the commercial data stream adds a separate question about access.

Jito does not sell the stream directly. Its distributors handle pricing, billing and onboarding.

Helius currently offers preconfirmations on Developer, Business, Professional and Enterprise plans, excluding free accounts. For each subscription in a slot that delivers at least one message, the charge is the greater of 300 credits or 10 credits per delivered message. Zero-message slots and uncovered slots cost nothing.

That means 10 delivered messages cost 300 credits, while 100 cost 1,000 credits, according to its published table. Concurrent subscriptions are billed separately. These are credit charges, not a stated dollar price per trade, and Helius says pricing is subject to change.

Customers also need to distinguish the two signals Helius includes by default. Its own preconfirmations appear when the leader executes a transaction and include its local execution status. BAM's arrive earlier, when the leader commits to execution, and carry no execution status in Helius's stream. Both precede shreds, but they describe different stages.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

A fixed execution sequence can coexist with paid access to earlier information.

The ordering guarantee concerns the committed batch. Preconfirmations are not finality. Sequence spacing is not time. The split is a published allocation, not measured payouts, profits or losses. Helius's own execution-stage signal is outside this BAM-only path.

Schematic of BAM's documented commitment-before-execution signal and partner distribution. A, B and C illustrate a batch, not actual trades. Revenue strip widths use the published 30/35/35 allocation on a 100% basis; no measured latency or earnings are drawn.

The ordering guarantee concerns the committed batch. Preconfirmations are not finality. Sequence spacing is not time. The split is a published allocation, not measured payouts, profits or losses. Helius's own execution-stage signal is outside this BAM-only path.

The commercial arrangement reaches the supply side too. BAM's published split assigns 35% of preconfirmation revenue to BAM validators, 35% to the Jito DAO and 30% to distributors.

Under the current validator rules, AgaveBAM and FireBAM operators are automatically opted into streaming. They can forgo the revenue and opt out, but self-service controls are not available yet. Opting out currently requires a ticket through Jito Developers Discord.

The validator portion is shared through eligible leader slots rather than paid solely to whichever validator produced a particular stream. BAM describes payments over the month after distributors pay the DAO, using priority fees attached to market-tick transactions. Skipped or disconnected slots earn nothing.

This gives participation a revenue incentive alongside an execution obligation. BAM says its Verifier checks produced blocks against commitments and that a validator that deviates can be disconnected.

BAM's operating model therefore puts a price on earlier information while enforcing a committed execution sequence. Assessing access means watching distributor eligibility, usage charges and actual stream coverage alongside those ordering safeguards. A fixed place in a batch does not by itself give every trader an equally early view.

"There is no excuse for what I did," Nvidia CEO says, as affair with an alleged Chinese spy makes the US-China AI race look even more ridiculous

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Just saying, Jensen, this probably doesn't happen if you stick to gaming GPUs

Ostensible graphics card company and definite AI giants Nvidia have commissioned an independent investigation into themselves, amid reports that CEO Jensen Huang had an extramarital affair with a suspected Chinese spy.

Per both the New York Times and Nvidia themselves, Nvidia offered to go under the microscope after US intelligence organisations elected to investigate Huang’s paramour, Chinese national Alice Huang (no, uh, relation, and also known as Huang Le), on suspicion of sharing sensitive information with Beijing. The Nvidia-running, leather-jacketed Huang has thus far only copped to an unspecified affair, admitting in an extraordinary public statement that he was "unfaithful to my wife." Still, the two investigations are likely to start a bizarre new leg in the alread-messy AI arms race that the US and China have been indulging in for years.

See, the basis for Huang and Huang’s relationship appears to be the same thing that’s been fucking us as PC owners: data centres and AI hardware. Alice Huang is a former managing director of Megaspeed, a Singapore-based data centre company who’ve bought billions of US dollars’ worth of Nvidia chips – and have been investigated for allegedly getting some of them into the hands of Chinese companies, in defiance of US trade restrictions on goods that might threaten the States’ AI dominance. Possibly also, given both countries’ increasing interest in the technology’s military uses, their perception of national security.

Many of these embargo-dodging accusations originate from after Alice Huang left Megaspeed, though she is now under that separate investigation for intelligence-sharing, and the prospect of any high-tech pillow talk from the lips of someone like Jensen Huang would be very disturbing to the feds indeed. Both Nvidia and their "deeply sorry" CEO deny that he shared sensitive information beyond that required for routine customer/client discussions.

"While the Board has no reason to believe that either national security or Nvidia secrets were compromised, the Board's inquiry will examine all available information," the company’s statement reads. "Jensen fully supports the review and has committed to cooperating with it. The Board has confidence in Jensen and his leadership team and will provide further updates as appropriate."

In the US-Sino battle of artificial intelligence, Jensen Huang has – as with his marriage – played both sides. He’s undoubtedly been a, perhaps the driving force behind reinventing Nvidia from beloved makers of gaming gear to the US’ number-one purveyors of environment-ruining, brain-polluting slop technology. But after the Biden administration banned American-made GPU exports to China over fears they’d be used to boost the latter’s military capabilities, Huang successfully lobbied Donald Trump into relaxing those restrictions, allowing Nvidia to flog their wares – including their crème de la crème AI GPU, the H200 – to cash-rich Chinese companies.

In a further twist, many of those chips have got mysteriously stuck in Hong Kong import facilities, suggesting the Chinese government in fact wants AI companies to stick to Chinese-made parts. But then, the Hangzhou-based DeepSeek reportedly trained their latest AI model on illicitly-gained Nvidia tech, so there’s either a healthy black market in operation, or the CCP is willing to tolerate the occasional bit of decadent Western silicone if it helps them catch up to the burger-chomping likes of ChatGPT and Claude. Maybe both, and either way, one can see how a well-placed honeypot could prove useful. Allegedly.

Without wanting to pick sides, in a race where I hope both teams lose, it’s also China who have more work to do. The People’s Republic produces a greater proportion of successful 'open' AI models, where the inner workings and even source code is made public, and mines more of the crucial minerals needed to build components and infrastructure. However, US-produced 'closed' models dominate global AI usage, and despite the lack of minerals, its existing data centres are estimated to wield 40 times the combined compute power of China’s. Probably because they just get all the RAM they need from Best Buy instead of digging it up themselves.

How to tell if your phone supports eSIM

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

If you bought your phone in the past few years, it likely has eSIM support.

Planning an international trip sounds fun until you realize just how much your phone bill will be. For such situations, eSIMs make more sense than physical SIMs. An eSIM is a digital SIM built directly into your device, allowing you to activate a carrier plan without inserting an actual physical nano-SIM card. For most people, that will even mean the ability to run two lines on one phone, as well as a faster setup process overall.

How can you tell whether your phone supports eSIM? The quickest way to check varies by operating system, but regardless of whether you have an iPhone or an Android device, you'll need to find something called EID. The presence of that code signals your phone supports eSIMs. On either platform, dialing *#06# from your phone app also pulls up the device information, including the EID.

Alternatively, if you're on an iPhone, go to Settings > General > About and look for an EID, which is a 32-digit number tied to your eSIM chip. Apple says you'll need to have an iPhone XS, XS Max, XR, or later to be able to add eSIMs. iPhone 13 and newer models can even run two eSIMs simultaneously.

On an Android phone, go to Settings > About phone and look under the Device details or Status information for the EID. You could also go to Settings > Network & Internet (or Connections on Samsung phones) > SIM manager to see if you have a way to add an eSIM.

Sadly, not all phones support eSIM. Android got eSIM support in 2017 and iPhones got it a year later. While the math is fairly easy with iOS devices since they're made by one company, things get a bit more complicated when it comes to Android manufacturers. For instance, the Google Pixel 2 and 2 XL got eSIM support in 2017, but Motorola only added support in 2019, and Samsung in 2020 with the Galaxy S20 series. Many budget or older Android models and some region-specific variants skip the embedded chip entirely, so the best course of action is to check if your phone has that EID code or not.

There's also the fact that your phone needs to be unlocked for it to actually work when you're on vacation. If your device is locked with your carrier, it may still show an EID code because you can technically get an eSIM from them, but it could refuse eSIM profiles from outside carriers, which will ruin your plans to use a random carrier for your trip abroad. To check this on an iPhone, go to Settings > General > About and look for "No SIM restrictions" next to Carrier Lock. Furthermore, you should confirm your carrier or service provider actually supports eSIM activation since not every provider has rolled out the feature, even if your phone has it.

If you're still unsure, you should contact your mobile network provider to confirm compatibility for your exact device. And if your phone does support eSIM, dual SIM functionality means you can often run a traditional SIM alongside it, which is useful for keeping a personal and work number, or a home line and a travel plan, active on a single phone.

London’s National Gallery publishes recommendations of its citizens’ assembly

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The recommendations comes as the museum plans for a major new extension

The National Gallery in London says in a new report that a new public body founded to advise the UK national museum on its future policy and direction has given it “a mandate to be brave”.

The new initiative, titled NG Citizens, was announced in July last year. The museum aims to transform its governance model by launching a citizens’ assembly made up of members of public from across the UK.

The accompanying report outlines the recommendations and shared vision developed by 55 participants who took part in five sessions between November 2025 and March 2026.

The new assembly recommended that the museum should “embrace and grow commercial opportunities” and also use technology to support personalised itineraries, especially for “under-represented” groups, among other objectives.

Other key recommendations for the museum include being more informative about its wider history, representation and context behind the art. The collection also urgently needs to be a true reflection of the UK, said the assembly members.

The overarching question put to the assembly members was: “What do you want from the UK’s National Gallery? How can it bring people and paintings together in authentic and meaningful ways, now and in the future?” A series of eight principles, defined as the “core values that guide how the gallery can move towards the vision”, were discussed at the sessions.

Core challenges on the agenda included: “How can the National Gallery balance its role as a free public gallery whilst being commercially minded? What is the social value of art? How can art galleries explain and support this? Can art ever be open to everyone?” Other issues discussed include “how to bring in new, diverse and loyal audiences.”

Over the next four years, a series of smaller panels, made up of assembly members, will work directly with the gallery to oversee the implementation of these ideas. “NG Citizens will now continue into its second phase: the Citizens’ Panel. The panel will consist of three separate cohorts between 2026 and 2030,” says the report.

Some issues were not up for discussion at the sessions says the report, such as ideas that would result in significant changes to existing buildings; ideas that involve buying or selling paintings; and ideas that might compromise the gallery’s role as an arm’s length body of the UK government.

The report concludes: “The National Gallery was the first of its kind to do this on this scale, in the world. We can accept it was never going to be perfect; it would present challenges, but we hope observers can learn from our experience… To have a group of people that we can work with over future years, who will maintain a critical and supportive perspective, presents opportunities far beyond the usual focus group approach.”

Maurice Davies, a UK culture consultant at Cultural Associates Oxford, says the report is “quite remarkable” but questions whether the National Gallery will be “bold and brave enough” to take forward the assembly’s recommendations.

He tells The Art Newspaper: “The panel have concluded that the gallery needs to be far more than it currently is; a collection of European paintings based in the centre of London. They say the collection ‘urgently’ needs to be far more diverse and to represent the diversity of the UK so it needs to change its collecting policy significantly.

And they say the gallery should share ‘paintings and resources’ throughout the UK which suggests the gallery might want to reconsider its current hugely expensive project to extend its building in central London in favour of using resources to work with museums and galleries in a wide range of locations.”

Last September, the National Gallery received pledges of £150m each (from Michael Moritz’s Crankstart foundation and the Julia Rausing Trust) for its planned major new extension scheduled for completion in the early 2030s.

His work will be made in collaboration with four institutions across the UK—from Northern Ireland to Scotland—before travelling to the capital

‘Connecting the dots’: Michael Saylor’s Strategy buys another 1,036 bitcoin for $82 million as holdings top 850,000 BTC

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Strategy has scooped up another 1,036 BTC for approximately $81.9 million at an average price of $79,012 per bitcoin.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) treasury company Strategy Strategy, Inc. (MSTR) bought another 1,036 BTC for $81.9 million at an average price of $79,012 per bitcoin between Nov. 2 and Nov. 8, according to a Monday filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission.

Strategy's bitcoin holdings now total 850,364 BTC, representing more than 4% of bitcoin's total supply cap. The firm's total holdings were acquired for a cost of approximately $64.2 billion at an average price of $75,456 per bitcoin, including fees and expenses.

Strategy used proceeds from at-the-market sales of its Class A common stock, MSTR, for the latest bitcoin acquisitions. Last week, Strategy sold 2,051,348 MSTR shares for approximately $283.8 million. Approximately $18.34 billion worth of MSTR shares remain available for issuance and sale under that ATM program, the firm said in the filing.

Additionally, the company also used its USD Reserve to repurchase 1,887,464 STRC preferred shares between Nov. 2 and Nov. 8 for roughly $188.3 million, and $2.1 million from the reserve to fund dividends on its preferred stocks.

As of Nov. 8, Strategy's USD Reserve and USD Cash balances were $4.77 billion and $146.2 million, respectively, according to the filing.

Strategy co-founder and Executive Chairman Michael Saylor posted a Strategy bitcoin acquisition tracker chart on Sunday, alongside the statement, "Connecting the dots," indicating another potential acquisition announcement ahead of Monday's filing.

Strategy remains by far the largest of nearly 200 public companies that have launched a bitcoin treasury model. Metaplanet, Tether-backed Twenty One, Strive, and MARA make up the remainder of the top five, with 44,000 BTC, 43,514 BTC, 43,124 BTC, and 35,577 BTC, respectively, according to data from Bitcoin Treasuries.

MSTR fell 2.1% last week, closing at $128.93 on Friday, according to The Block's Strategy price page, while bitcoin slipped 3.2% during the same period.
